Use when the user runs /design-sync or says \"sync my design system to Claude Design\".","argumentHint":"[<project hint, e.g. \"Acme DS\">]","builtin":true},{"name":"dataviz","description":"Use this skill whenever you are about to create ANY chart, graph, plot, dashboard, or data visualization, in ANY output medium — an HTML or React artifact, inline SVG, plotting code in any library (matplotlib, plotly, d3, Recharts, …), an image/PNG you will render and upload, or a chart shared into Slack. Read it BEFORE writing the first line of chart code, choosing chart colors, building a stat tile / meter / KPI row, or laying out a dashboard. When the destination is a first-party document connector (host-designated, never self-described) that renders live charts, hand it the rows (inline, or as an uploaded data file the chart cites) rather than a rendered PNG/SVG — a picture of a chart loses hover, data inspection and per-value comments. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. Triggers on: \"chart\", \"graph\", \"plot\", \"data viz\", \"visualization\", \"dashboard\", \"analytics\", \"visualize data\", \"categorical colors\", \"sequential / diverging palette\", \"stat tile\", \"sparkline\", \"heatmap\", \"legend\", \"axis\", \"tooltip\", \"chart colors\", \"color by series\".","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"update-config","description":"Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"verify","description":"Verify that a code change actually does what it's supposed to by exercising it end-to-end and observing behavior — drive the affected flow, not just tests or typecheck. Run before committing nontrivial changes; bootstraps this repo's project verify skill if none exists yet. Don't invoke it on a diff that only touches tests, docs, or other code with no runtime surface to drive (a change to product source always has one) — there's nothing to observe.","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"debug","description":"Enable debug logging for this session and help diagnose issues","argumentHint":"[issue description]","builtin":true},{"name":"code-review","description":"Review the current diff, or a PR number/branch/path target, for correctness bugs (plus reuse/simplification/efficiency cleanups where the model's review recipe covers them) at the given effort level (low/medium: fewer, high-confidence findings; high→max: broader coverage, may include uncertain findings; ultra: deep multi-agent review in the cloud); with no level given, it reuses the level you typed last. Pass --comment to post findings as inline PR comments, or --fix to apply the findings to the working tree after the review. For ultra on a GitHub.com PR target, --post asks to post the finished review’s findings to the PR as a single comment from the user’s GitHub account (not a review; the launch dialog still confirms in interactive sessions, while non-interactive mode posts on the flag alone) and --no-post hides that option.","argumentHint":"[low|medium|high|xhigh|max|ultra] [--fix] [--comment] [<pr#>|<branch>|<path>]","aliases":["review"],"builtin":true},{"name":"simplify","description":"Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.","argumentHint":"[<target>]","builtin":true},{"name":"batch","description":"Research and plan a large-scale change, then execute it in parallel across 5–30 isolated worktree agents that each open a PR.","argumentHint":"<instruction>","builtin":true},{"name":"fewer-permission-prompts","description":"Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"doctor","description":"Health-check the user's Claude Code setup and fix issues: diagnose installation health — what the `claude doctor` terminal diagnostics cover — from local data (duplicate or leftover installs, PATH, unparseable settings files, broken or colliding agent definitions, skills whose frontmatter fails to parse); find unused skills, MCP servers, and plugins versus their context cost and disable dead weight; deduplicate local CLAUDE.md files against checked-in ones; trim checked-in CLAUDE.md files by cutting content a session could derive from the codebase (directory layouts, tech-stack lists, architecture overviews) while keeping gotchas, rationale, and non-standard conventions; migrate always-loaded CLAUDE.md guidance into lazy skills and nested CLAUDE.md files; flag slow hooks and context-heavy extensions; check the installed version is current; make auto mode the default permission mode; and pre-approve frequently denied read-only commands. Use when the user asks for a doctor run, checkup, audit, tune-up, or cleanup of their Claude Code setup or configuration.","argumentHint":"","aliases":["checkup"],"builtin":true},{"name":"loop","description":"Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). and mcp__remote-devices__Claude_Browser__* when a cloud session is linked to the person's computer; before those tools are turned on there may be a single enable__mcp__remote-devices__Claude_Browser tool instead. It covers the pane's persistent sign-ins, tabs and preview_start, reading pages as text, site approvals, what the pane cannot open, and what to do when it cannot be reached. It is not for Claude in Chrome (mcp__claude-in-chrome__* tools), which has its own skill, and it does not decide which browser to use. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["built-in-browser"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:chrome-browser","description":"Read this skill before the first step that uses Claude in Chrome, the browser extension whose tools are named mcp__claude-in-chrome__* (also called Chrome, the browser extension, or the external browser) and which acts in the person's real Chrome with their own sign-ins; before those tools are turned on there may be a single enable__mcp__claude-in-chrome tool instead. It covers loading the tools in one ToolSearch call, checking the person's open tabs and working in a new tab, site permissions, GIF recordings, console logs, dialogs to avoid, and when to stop and ask. It is not for the built-in browser (mcp__Claude_Browser__* or mcp__remote-devices__Claude_Browser__* tools), which has its own skill, and it does not decide which browser to use. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["chrome-browser"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:computer-use","description":"Read this skill before the first step of any request to do something in an app on the person's own computer (Notes, Finder, System Settings, any desktop app), to look at their screen, or for \"computer use\". Computer use (desktop control) lets Claude take screenshots of the person's desktop and control it with clicks, typing and scrolling through the Claude desktop app; its tools are named mcp__computer-use__* when the session runs in the desktop app and mcp__remote-devices__computer_* when a cloud session is linked to the person's computer; before computer use is turned on for a conversation there may be no such tools, only an enable__mcp__remote-devices__computer tool, which turns it on. It covers turning it on, picking the right tool, the access flow, and the safety rules for tiered apps, links and financial actions. It is not for websites, which go through Claude in Chrome or the built-in browser and their own skills. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["computer-use"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:deep-research","description":"Use this skill when the user's prompt requires (1) researching a topic across multiple sources, comparing options or alternatives, analyzing trends or history, understanding markets or industries, or reviewing literature or studies and (2) synthesizing that research into a comprehensive, narrative report. If you're planning to search the web or internal knowledge bases, consider using this skill. This skill coordinates research subagents, so use it only when you have a tool for spawning subagents (the Agent or Task tool); otherwise, research the question directly. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":""},{"name":"anthropic-skills:docs","description":"docs (living docs people share, comment on and edit; use only when the user asks for one: names a doc, document, page, memo, spec, PRD, runbook or write-up, asks for somewhere to share or keep editing something, or says yes to your doc offer; a plan, comparison, summary or notes asked in chat stays in chat (at most a one-line doc offer); a report, status update, recap or \"something I can send them\" with no form named → ask first: reply, doc or file?; tabs hold tables and live charts too; a pasted claude.ai/code/artifact/… link may be a doc: check with docs tools first; not HTML pages, apps or plain chat answers; a .docx/.pptx/.xlsx/PDF asked for by name → that format's skill): asked for one → no docs-connector instructions in context? call the docs connector's `guide` with topic.instructions first, then create the doc (headings only, no body) before any search, file read or plan, even with files attached. Documenting code means docstrings or repo docs, not a doc. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["docs"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:docx","description":"Use this skill whenever the user wants to create, read, edit, or manipulate Word documents (.docx) or Word templates (.dotx). Triggers include: any mention of 'Word doc', 'word document', '.docx', '.dotx', or requests to produce professional documents with formatting like tables of contents, page numbers, or letterheads. Also use when extracting or reorganizing content from .docx or .dotx files, inserting or replacing images in documents, find-and-replace in Word files, working with tracked changes or comments, or converting content into a polished Word document. If the user asks for a 'report', 'memo', 'letter', 'template', or similar deliverable as a Word or .docx file (to download, email or print), use this skill. However, if they ask for a document, page, report, memo, or notes WITHOUT naming a file format and the session offers Claude's own dedicated document or page skill or connector, use that instead. Do NOT use for PDFs, spreadsheets, Google Docs, or coding unrelated to document generation. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["docx"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:import-memory","description":"Import a memory export from another AI assistant into Claude's memory — conversationally, additively, and with the content treated as data. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["import-memory"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:morning","description":"Render the user's morning brief as a styled HTML artifact, or set it up as a recurring weekday task. Use only when the user explicitly asks to run, see, or set up their morning brief, or if they invoke /morning by name. A question about their day, schedule, or calendar is not by itself a request for the brief; answer it directly instead. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["morning"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:pdf","description":"Use this skill whenever the user wants to do anything with PDF files. This includes reading or extracting text/tables from PDFs, combining or merging multiple PDFs into one, splitting PDFs apart, rotating pages, adding watermarks, creating new PDFs, filling PDF forms, encrypting/decrypting PDFs, extracting images, and OCR on scanned PDFs to make them searchable. If the user mentions a .pdf file or asks to produce one, use this skill. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["pdf"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:pptx","description":"Use this skill any time a .pptx or .potx file is involved in any way — as input, output, or both. This includes: creating slide decks, pitch decks, or presentations as PowerPoint (.pptx) files; reading, parsing, or extracting text from any .pptx or .potx file (even if the extracted content will be used elsewhere, like in an email, summary, or creating a different type of slide deck); editing, modifying, or updating existing presentations; combining or splitting slide files; working with templates (.potx), layouts, speaker notes, or comments. Trigger whenever the user asks for a PowerPoint or .pptx file, or references a .pptx or .potx filename, regardless of what they plan to do with the content afterward. However, when the user asks for a deck, slides, a slide deck, or a presentation without naming a file format, default to using a dedicated slide-deck artifact type or a separate slides skill if this session offers one; otherwise, use this skill. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["pptx"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:setup-writing-style","description":"Learns how the user writes from their own sent messages and docs, and builds a voice profile so future drafts sound like them instead of generic AI. The profile is saved as the my-writing-style skill. Use when the user asks to set up, learn, or capture their writing voice, or complains that drafts sound generic or unlike them and no my-writing-style profile exists. Only for drafting text the user will send as themselves, not for Claude's own replies. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["setup-writing-style"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:skill-creator","description":"Create new skills, modify and improve existing skills, and measure skill performance. Use when users want to create a skill from scratch, edit, or optimize an existing skill, run evals to test a skill, benchmark skill performance with variance analysis, or optimize a skill's description for better triggering accuracy. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["skill-creator"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:xlsx","description":"Use this skill any time a spreadsheet file is the primary input or output. This means any task where the user wants to: open, read, edit, or fix an existing .xlsx, .xlsm, .xltx, .csv, or .tsv file (e.g., adding columns, computing formulas, formatting, charting, cleaning messy data); create a new spreadsheet from scratch or from other data sources; or convert between tabular file formats. Trigger especially when the user references a spreadsheet file by name or path — even casually (like \"the xlsx in my downloads\") — and wants something done to it or produced from it. Also trigger for cleaning or restructuring messy tabular data files (malformed rows, misplaced headers, junk data) into proper spreadsheets. The deliverable must be a spreadsheet file. Do NOT trigger when the primary deliverable is a Word document, HTML report, standalone Python script, database pipeline, or Google Sheets API integration, even if tabular data is involved. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["xlsx"]}],"uuid":"e8fcb787-d6e3-4a9d-bf86-f515ce345443","session_id":"ec9d537e-1101-467b-b8cb-81912926f03f"}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Gfegk5tQaSnwFcNpnzAXAf","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t/**\n2\t * GraphQL Authentication Guard\n3\t *\n4\t * Validates JWT tokens for GraphQL requests in hr-service.\n5\t * Provides defense-in-depth authentication at the resolver level.\n6\t *\n7\t * @module Common/Guards\n8\t */\n9\timport {\n10\t  Injectable,\n11\t  Inject,\n12\t  CanActivate,\n13\t  ExecutionContext,\n14\t  UnauthorizedException,\n15\t  Logger,\n16\t  SetMetadata,\n17\t} from '@nestjs/common';\n18\timport { ConfigService } from '@nestjs/config';\n19\timport { Reflector } from '@nestjs/core';\n20\timport { GqlExecutionContext } from '@nestjs/graphql';\n21\timport { JwtService } from '@nestjs/jwt';\n22\timport { Request } from 'express';\n23\timport { getJwtVerifyOptions, enforceAccessTokenType } from '@aquaculture/backend-common/auth';\n24\t\n25\t/**\n26\t * Metadata key for public routes\n27\t */\n28\texport const IS_PUBLIC_KEY = 'isPublic';\n29\t\n30\t/**\n31\t * Public decorator - marks a route as publicly accessible\n32\t */\n33\texport const Public = (): ReturnType<typeof SetMetadata> =>\n34\t  SetMetadata(IS_PUBLIC_KEY, true);\n35\t\n36\t/**\n37\t * JWT Payload interface\n38\t */\n39\texport interface JwtPayload {\n40\t  sub: string;\n41\t  email: string;\n42\t  tenantId: string;\n43\t  roles: string[];\n44\t  permissions?: string[];\n45\t  iat: number;\n46\t  exp: number;\n47\t}\n48\t\n49\t/**\n50\t * Authenticated request with user info\n51\t */\n52\tinterface AuthenticatedRequest extends Request {\n53\t  user?: JwtPayload;\n54\t  userId?: string;\n55\t  tenantId?: string;\n56\t}\n57\t\n58\t/**\n59\t * GraphQL context with request\n60\t */\n61\tinterface GqlContext {\n62\t  req?: AuthenticatedRequest;\n63\t}\n64\t\n65\t/**\n66\t * GqlAuthGuard\n67\t * Validates JWT tokens for GraphQL requests\n68\t * Supports public routes via @Public() decorator\n69\t */\n70\t@Injectable()\n71\texport class GqlAuthGuard implements CanActivate {\n72\t  private readonly logger = new Logger(GqlAuthGuard.name);\n73\t\n74\t  // WHY: Explicit @Inject() — design:paramtypes may not survive all build/runtime environments.\n75\t  constructor(\n76\t    @Inject(JwtService) private readonly jwtService: JwtService,\n77\t    @Inject(ConfigService) private readonly configService: ConfigService,\n78\t    @Inject(Reflector) private readonly reflector: Reflector,\n79\t  ) {}\n80\t\n81\t  async canActivate(context: ExecutionContext): Promise<boolean> {\n82\t    // Check if route is marked as public\n83\t    const isPublic = this.reflector.getAllAndOverride<boolean>(IS_PUBLIC_KEY, [\n84\t      context.getHandler(),\n85\t      context.getClass(),\n86\t    ]);\n87\t\n88\t    if (isPublic) {\n89\t      return true;\n90\t    }\n91\t\n92\t    // Get GraphQL context\n93\t    const ctx = GqlExecutionContext.create(context);\n94\t    const gqlContext = ctx.getContext<GqlContext>();\n95\t    const request = gqlContext.req;\n96\t\n97\t    if (!request) {\n98\t      // REST endpoint fallback\n99\t      const httpContext = context.switchToHttp();\n100\t      const httpRequest = httpContext.getRequest<AuthenticatedRequest>();\n101\t      return this.validateRequest(httpRequest);\n102\t    }\n103\t\n104\t    return this.validateRequest(request);\n105\t  }\n106\t\n107\t  private async validateRequest(\n108\t    request: AuthenticatedRequest,\n109\t  ): Promise<boolean> {\n110\t    // SECURITY (HIGH-04): request.user is set by UserContextMiddleware which parses the\n111\t    // x-user-payload header injected by the gateway. This is trusted only because the\n112\t    // gateway is assumed to be the sole entry point (network-level isolation).\n113\t    // Defence-in-depth: require that a pre-set user also carries a non-empty roles array,\n114\t    // which is always present in a genuine gateway-injected JWT payload but absent if an\n115\t    // attacker crafts a minimal x-user-payload header manually.\n116\t    if (\n117\t      request.user &&\n118\t      request.user.sub &&\n119\t      request.user.tenantId &&\n120\t      Array.isArray((request.user as JwtPayload).roles) &&\n121\t      (request.user as JwtPayload).roles.length > 0\n122\t    ) {\n123\t      this.logger.debug(\n124\t        `User ${request.user.sub} pre-authenticated for tenant ${request.user.tenantId}`,\n125\t      );\n126\t      return true;\n127\t    }\n128\t\n129\t    const token = this.extractToken(request);\n130\t\n131\t    if (!token) {\n132\t      throw new UnauthorizedException('Missing authentication token');\n133\t    }\n134\t\n135\t    try {\n136\t      // Use centralised JWT verification options from backend-common.\n137\t      // BEFORE: verifyAsync() called with only { secret } — no algorithm restriction.\n138\t      // Algorithm confusion attacks (RS256 downgrade) were possible.\n139\t      // Tokens without iss/aud claims were silently accepted (no conditional checks here).\n140\t      // AFTER: getJwtVerifyOptions() enforces all three at jsonwebtoken library level.\n141\t      const payload = await this.jwtService.verifyAsync<JwtPayload>(\n142\t        token,\n143\t        getJwtVerifyOptions(this.configService),\n144\t      );\n145\t\n146\t      // Defense-in-depth: subgraph guard also enforces token type.\n147\t      const isProduction = this.configService.get<string>('NODE_ENV') === 'production';\n148\t      enforceAccessTokenType(payload, this.logger, isProduction);\n149\t\n150\t      // Attach user info to request\n151\t      request.user = payload;\n152\t      request.userId = payload.sub;\n153\t      request.tenantId = payload.tenantId;\n154\t\n155\t      this.logger.debug(\n156\t        `User ${payload.sub} * GraphQL Authentication Guard\n *\n * Validates JWT tokens for GraphQL requests in hr-service.\n * Provides defense-in-depth authentication at the resolver level.\n *\n * @module Common/Guards\n */\nimport {\n  Injectable,\n  Inject,\n  CanActivate,\n  ExecutionContext,\n  UnauthorizedException,\n  Logger,\n  SetMetadata,\n} from '@nestjs/common';\nimport { ConfigService } from '@nestjs/config';\nimport { Reflector } from '@nestjs/core';\nimport { GqlExecutionContext } from '@nestjs/graphql';\nimport { JwtService } from '@nestjs/jwt';\nimport { Request } from 'express';\nimport { getJwtVerifyOptions, enforceAccessTokenType } from '@aquaculture/backend-common/auth';\n\n/**\n * Metadata key for public routes\n */\nexport const IS_PUBLIC_KEY = 'isPublic';\n\n/**\n * Public decorator - marks a route as publicly accessible\n */\nexport const Public = (): ReturnType<typeof SetMetadata> =>\n  SetMetadata(IS_PUBLIC_KEY, true);\n\n/**\n * JWT Payload interface\n */\nexport interface JwtPayload {\n  sub: string;\n  email: string;\n  tenantId: string;\n  roles: string[];\n  permissions?: string[];\n  iat: number;\n  exp: number;\n}\n\n/**\n * Authenticated request with user info\n */\ninterface AuthenticatedRequest extends Request {\n  user?: JwtPayload;\n  userId?: string;\n  tenantId?: string;\n}\n\n/**\n * GraphQL context with request\n */\ninterface GqlContext {\n  req?: AuthenticatedRequest;\n}\n\n/**\n * GqlAuthGuard\n * Validates JWT tokens for GraphQL requests\n * Supports public routes via @Public() decorator\n */\n@Injectable()\nexport class GqlAuthGuard implements CanActivate {\n  private readonly logger = new Logger(GqlAuthGuard.name);\n\n  // WHY: Explicit @Inject() — design:paramtypes may not survive all build/runtime environments.\n  constructor(\n    @Inject(JwtService) private readonly jwtService: JwtService,\n    @Inject(ConfigService) private readonly configService: ConfigService,\n    @Inject(Reflector) private readonly reflector: Reflector,\n  ) {}\n\n  async canActivate(context: ExecutionContext): Promise<boolean> {\n    // Check if route is marked as public\n    const isPublic = this.reflector.getAllAndOverride<boolean>(IS_PUBLIC_KEY, [\n      context.getHandler(),\n      context.getClass(),\n    ]);\n\n    if (isPublic) {\n      return true;\n    }\n\n    // Get GraphQL context\n    const ctx = GqlExecutionContext.create(context);\n    const gqlContext = ctx.getContext<GqlContext>();\n    const request = gqlContext.req;\n\n    if (!request) {\n      // REST endpoint fallback\n      const httpContext = context.switchToHttp();\n      const httpRequest = httpContext.getRequest<AuthenticatedRequest>();\n      return this.validateRequest(httpRequest);\n    }\n\n    return this.validateRequest(request);\n  }\n\n  private async validateRequest(\n    request: AuthenticatedRequest,\n  ): Promise<boolean> {\n    // SECURITY (HIGH-04): request.user is set by UserContextMiddleware which parses the\n    // x-user-payload header injected by the gateway. This is trusted only because the\n    // gateway is assumed to be the sole entry point (network-level isolation).\n    // Defence-in-depth: require that a pre-set user also carries a non-empty roles array,\n    // which is always present in a genuine gateway-injected JWT payload but absent if an\n    // attacker crafts a minimal x-user-payload header manually.\n    if (\n      request.user &&\n      request.user.sub &&\n      request.user.tenantId &&\n      Array.isArray((request.user as JwtPayload).roles) &&\n      (request.user as JwtPayload).roles.length > 0\n    ) {\n      this.logger.debug(\n        `User ${request.user.sub} pre-authenticated for tenant ${request.user.tenantId}`,\n      );\n      return true;\n    }\n\n    const token = this.extractToken(request);\n\n    if (!token) {\n      throw new UnauthorizedException('Missing authentication token');\n    }\n\n    try {\n      // Use centralised JWT verification options from backend-common.\n      // BEFORE: verifyAsync() called with only { secret } — no algorithm restriction.\n      // Algorithm confusion attacks (RS256 downgrade) were possible.\n      // Tokens without iss/aud claims were silently accepted (no conditional checks here).\n      // AFTER: getJwtVerifyOptions() enforces all three at jsonwebtoken library level.\n      const payload = await this.jwtService.verifyAsync<JwtPayload>(\n        token,\n        getJwtVerifyOptions(this.configService),\n      );\n\n      // Defense-in-depth: subgraph guard also enforces token type.\n      const isProduction = this.configService.get<string>('NODE_ENV') === 'production';\n      enforceAccessTokenType(payload, this.logger, isProduction);\n\n      // Attach user info to request\n      request.user = payload;\n      request.userId = payload.sub;\n      request.tenantId = payload.tenantId;\n\n      this.logger.debug(\n        `User ${payload.sub} authenticated for tenant ${payload.tenantId}`,\n      );\n\n      return true;\n    } catch (error) {\n      this.logger.warn(`JWT validation failed: ${(error as Error).message}`);\n      throw new UnauthorizedException('Invalid or expired token');\n    }\n  }\n\n  private extractToken(request: AuthenticatedRequest): string | null {\n    const authHeader = request.headers?.authorization;\n\n    if (!authHeader) {\n      return null;\n    }\n\n    const parts = authHeader.split(' ');\n    const type = parts[0];\n    const token = parts[1];\n\n    if (type !== 'Bearer' || !token) {\n      return null;\n    }\n\n    return token;\n  }\n}\n","numLines":184,"startLine":1,"totalLines":184}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01XLXbxgD4hwMYCocWX4JsPc","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t/**\n2\t * Platform-wide invariant — SEC-HIGH-156:\n3\t *\n4\t * Every tenant-scoped subgraph AppModule MUST mount VerifiedUserAssertionMiddleware\n5\t * via MiddlewareConsumer.apply(...) in its configure() method, AFTER\n6\t * StripInternalHeadersMiddleware (which sets req.verifiedIdentity) and BEFORE\n7\t * UserContextMiddleware. other\n13\t * seven tenant-scoped subgraphs leaned on the legacy path, so a compromised\n14\t * intermediary that stripped/forged the tenant header (without the bound\n15\t * assertion) was a larger surface than necessary.\n16\t *\n17\t * # Source-level, same rationale as public-service-edge-hardening.spec.ts:\n18\t * one grep+position pass over every service fires on every PR — fastest\n19\t * gateway-federated, tenant-bearing\n31\t * GraphQL and therefore MUST resolve identity from the signed assertion.\n32\t * (gateway-api is the SIGNER; auth-service ISSUES the JWT — neither receives\n33\t * an assertion, so both are intentionally absent.)\n34\t */\n35\tconst SUBGRAPHS_REQUIRED: ReadonlyArray<string> = [\n36\t  'farm-service',\n37\t  'config-service',\n38\t  'billing-service',\n39\t  'sensor-service',\n40\t  'hr-service',\n41\t  'hydroponics-service',\n42\t  'alert-engine',\n43\t  'messaging-service',\n44\t  'ai-service',\n45\t];\n46\t\n47\tinterface ModuleAnalysis {\n48\t  service: string;\n49\t  hasImport: boolean;\n50\t  importsFromCanonical: boolean;\n51\t  hasApplyMount: boolean;\n52\t  /** Strip < VerifiedUserAssertion < UserContext (UserContext optional). */\n53\t  orderedCorrectly: boolean;\n54\t}\n55\t\n56\tfunction analyzeAppModule(service: string): ModuleAnalysis | null {\n57\t  const path = `apps/${service}/src/app.module.ts`;\n58\t  let src: string;\n59\t  try {\n60\t    src = readFileSync(resolve(REPO_ROOT, path), 'utf8');\n61\t  } catch {\n62\t    return null;\n63\t  }\n64\t  // Drop block + line comments so docstring mentions of the middleware names do\n65\t  // not register as positional code occurrences.\n66\t  const stripped = src\n67\t    .replace(/\\/\\*[\\s\\S]*?\\*\\//g, '')\n68\t    .split('\\n')\n69\t    .map((line) => line.replace(/(?<!:)\\/\\/.*$/, ''))\n70\t    .join('\\n');\n71\t\n72\t  const M = 'VerifiedUserAssertionMiddleware';\n73\t  return {\n74\t `import\\\\s+\\\\{[^}]*\\\\b${M}\\\\b[^}]*\\\\}\\\\s+from\\\\s+['\"]@aquaculture/backend-common/middleware['\"]`,\n78\t    ).test(stripped),\n79\t    hasApplyMount: new * Platform-wide invariant — SEC-HIGH-156:\n *\n * Every tenant-scoped subgraph AppModule MUST mount VerifiedUserAssertionMiddleware\n * via MiddlewareConsumer.apply(...) in its configure() method, AFTER\n * StripInternalHeadersMiddleware (which sets req.verifiedIdentity) and BEFORE\n * UserContextMiddleware. This makes the gateway-signed x-verified-user-assertion\n * (which binds the user AND the effective tenant into one HMAC-signed blob) the\n * single source of req.user / req.tenantId on every subgraph — instead of the\n * weaker legacy path (raw JWT + a separately-trusted x-tenant-id header).\n *\n * Before this invariant only farm + config mounted the middleware; the other\n * seven tenant-scoped subgraphs leaned on the legacy path, so a compromised\n * intermediary that stripped/forged the tenant header (without the bound\n * assertion) was a larger surface than necessary.\n *\n * # Source-level, same rationale as public-service-edge-hardening.spec.ts:\n * one grep+position pass over every service fires on every PR — fastest\n * feedback for a class of regression that would otherwise ship as a silently\n * weaker trust boundary.\n */\n\nimport { execFileSync } from 'node:child_process';\nimport { readFileSync } from 'node:fs';\nimport { resolve } from 'node:path';\n\nconst REPO_ROOT = resolve(__dirname, '..', '..');\n\n/**\n * Tenant-scoped subgraphs that receive gateway-federated, tenant-bearing\n * GraphQL and therefore MUST resolve identity from the signed assertion.\n * (gateway-api is the SIGNER; auth-service ISSUES the JWT — neither receives\n * an assertion, so both are intentionally absent.)\n */\nconst SUBGRAPHS_REQUIRED: ReadonlyArray<string> = [\n  'farm-service',\n  'config-service',\n  'billing-service',\n  'sensor-service',\n  'hr-service',\n  'hydroponics-service',\n  'alert-engine',\n  'messaging-service',\n  'ai-service',\n];\n\ninterface ModuleAnalysis {\n  service: string;\n  hasImport: boolean;\n  importsFromCanonical: boolean;\n  hasApplyMount: boolean;\n  /** Strip < VerifiedUserAssertion < UserContext (UserContext optional). */\n  orderedCorrectly: boolean;\n}\n\nfunction analyzeAppModule(service: string): ModuleAnalysis | null {\n  const path = `apps/${service}/src/app.module.ts`;\n  let src: string;\n  try {\n    src = readFileSync(resolve(REPO_ROOT, path), 'utf8');\n  } catch {\n    return null;\n  }\n  // Drop block + line comments so docstring mentions of the middleware names do\n  // not register as positional code occurrences.\n  const stripped = src\n    .replace(/\\/\\*[\\s\\S]*?\\*\\//g, '')\n    .split('\\n')\n    .map((line) => line.replace(/(?<!:)\\/\\/.*$/, ''))\n    .join('\\n');\n\n  const M = 'VerifiedUserAssertionMiddleware';\n  return {\n    service,\n    hasImport: new RegExp(`import\\\\s+\\\\{[^}]*\\\\b${M}\\\\b[^}]*\\\\}`).test(stripped),\n    importsFromCanonical: new RegExp(\n      `import\\\\s+\\\\{[^}]*\\\\b${M}\\\\b[^}]*\\\\}\\\\s+from\\\\s+['\"]@aquaculture/backend-common/middleware['\"]`,\n    ).test(stripped),\n    hasApplyMount: new RegExp(`\\\\.apply\\\\([\\\\s\\\\S]*?\\\\b${M}\\\\b`).test(stripped),\n    orderedCorrectly: (() => {","numLines":80,"startLine":1,"totalLines":155}}}

'EC1',\n36\t      country: 'UK',\n37\t    },\n38\t    ...over,\n39\t  } as Employee;\n40\t}\n41\t\n42\tfunction makeViewer(roles: string[], sub = 'viewer-user'): CurrentUserPayload {\n43\t  return { sub, email: 'v@farm.test', tenantId: 't1', roles } as CurrentUserPayload;\n44\t}\n45\t\n46\tfunction makeResolver(employee: Employee) {\n47\t  const queryBus = { execute: jest.fn().mockResolvedValue(employee) };\n48\t  const commandBus = { execute: jest.fn() };\n49\t  return new HRResolver(commandBus as never, queryBus as never);\n50\t}\n51\t\n52\tdescribe('HRResolver employee contact-PII masking', () => {\n53\t  it('TENANT_ADMIN sees full contact PII', async () => {\n54\t    const emp = makeEmployee();\n55\t    const resolver = makeResolver(emp);\n56\t    const result = await resolver.getEmployee('emp-1', context, makeViewer([Role.TENANT_ADMIN]));\n57\t    expect(result.contactInfo.phone).toBe('+1-555-0100');\n58\t    expect(result.address.street).toBe('12 Analytical Ave');\n59\t  });\n60\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"ec9d537e-1101-467b-b8cb-81912926f03f","uuid":"c53c1b5b-f917-4705-a71f-3ef54bef3b17","timestamp":"2026-09-19T22:06:24.441Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-evidence-judge-a0bd4b0b5f2a/apps/hr-service/src/hr/__tests__/hr.resolver.employee-pii.spec.ts","content":"/**\n * HRResolver — employee contact-PII object-level authz (DB-PEOPLE-MEDIUM-001).\n *\n * The employee read queries are gated to the broad MODULE_USER role. Before this\n * fix, any MODULE_USER could read every colleague's home address, personal\n * phone, and emergency contacts. These tests pin that full contact PII reaches\n * only workforce managers (TENANT_ADMIN/MODULE_MANAGER) or the subject\n * themselves; everyone else gets a redacted directory projection.\n */\nimport { HRResolver } from '../hr.resolver';\nimport { Role } from '@aquaculture/backend-common/decorators';\nimport type { CurrentUserPayload } from '@aquaculture/backend-common/decorators';\nimport { Employee } from '../entities/employee.entity';\n\nconst context = { req: { user: { sub: 'viewer-user', tenantId: 't1' } } } as never;\n\nfunction makeEmployee(over: Partial<Employee> = {}): Employee {\n  return {\n    id: 'emp-1',\n    tenantId: 't1',\n    userId: 'other-user',\n    firstName: 'Ada',\n    lastName: 'Lovelace',\n    email: 'ada@farm.test',\n    contactInfo: {\n      email: 'ada@farm.test',\n      phone: '+1-555-0100',\n      emergencyContact: 'Charles',\n      emergencyPhone: '+1-555-0199',\n    },\n    address: {\n      street: '12 Analytical Ave',\n      city: 'London',\n      state: 'LDN',\n      postalCode: 'EC1',\n      country: 'UK',\n    },\n    ...over,\n  } as Employee;\n}\n\nfunction makeViewer(roles: string[], sub = 'viewer-user'): CurrentUserPayload {\n  return { sub, email: 'v@farm.test', tenantId: 't1', roles } as CurrentUserPayload;\n}\n\nfunction makeResolver(employee: Employee) {\n  const queryBus = { execute: jest.fn().mockResolvedValue(employee) };\n  const commandBus = { execute: jest.fn() };\n  return new HRResolver(commandBus as never, queryBus as never);\n}\n\ndescribe('HRResolver employee contact-PII masking', () => {\n  it('TENANT_ADMIN sees full contact PII', async () => {\n    const emp = makeEmployee();\n    const resolver = makeResolver(emp);\n    const result = await resolver.getEmployee('emp-1', context, makeViewer([Role.TENANT_ADMIN]));\n    expect(result.contactInfo.phone).toBe('+1-555-0100');\n    expect(result.address.street).toBe('12 Analytical Ave');\n  });\n","numLines":60,"startLine":1,"totalLines":109}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_011DMgWFB7kRtA1nYPntHm1p","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t/**\n2\t * INVARIANT — no dead guards (ADR-0010, SEC-HIGH-060).\n3\t *\n4\t * `IpWhitelistGuard` sat in gateway-api for two years registered in no\n5\t * module, under no `APP_GUARD`, in no `@UseGuards()`. It read as an access\n6\t * control and controlled nothing. A guard class that exists but is wired to\n7\t * no request path is worse than no guard: reviewers, auditors and the next\n8\t * engineer all read it as protection.\n9\t *\n10\t * Rule: every non-abstract class implementing `CanActivate` under `apps/**`\n11\t * and `libs/**` (production source, not tests) must be reachable from a\n12\t * registration site:\n13\t *   - an `APP_GUARD` provider (`useClass`, `useExisting`, or a `useFactory`\n14\t *     that constructs it),\n15\t *   - a `@UseGuards(...)` / `UseGuards(...)` call (controller, resolver,\n16\t *     handler, or an `applyDecorators` bundle),\n17\t *   - a `globalGuards: [...]` / `useGlobalGuards(...)` bootstrap list,\n18\t *   - or a registered guard that `extends` it,\n19\t * or carry an entry in `.claude/allowlists/unregistered-guards.yaml` with\n20\t * `{owner, expiry, findingId, reason}`. Expired entries fail; entries whose\n21\t * class no longer exists fail (the list only shrinks).\n22\t *\n23\t * The second block keeps both retired IP access-rule stacks deleted.\n24\t */\n25\t\n26\timport { execFileSync } from 'node:child_process';\n27\timport { readFileSync } from 'node:fs';\n28\timport { resolve } from 'node:path';\n29\t\n30\timport * as yaml from 'js-yaml';\n31\t\n32\tconst REPO_ROOT = resolve(__dirname, '..', '..');\n33\tconst ALLOWLIST = resolve(REPO_ROOT, '.claude/allowlists/unregistered-guards.yaml');\n34\t\n35\t/** Production source only: tests, fixtures and built output are not registration evidence. */\n36\tconst NOT_PRODUCTION =\n37\t  /(^|\\/)(__tests__|__mocks__|test|tests|e2e|dist|\\.archive)\\/|\\.(spec|test)\\.tsx?$/;\n38\t\n39\tfunction listSourceFiles(): string[] {\n40\t  return execFileSync(\n41\t    'git',\n42\t    [\n43\t      '-C',\n44\t      REPO_ROOT,\n45\t      'ls-files',\n46\t      '--cached',\n47\t      '--others',\n48\t      '--exclude-standard',\n49\t      '--',\n50\t      'apps',\n51\t      'libs',\n52\t      'platform',\n53\t    ],\n54\t    { encoding: 'utf8', maxBuffer: 64 * 1024 * 1024 },\n55\t  )\n56\t    .split('\\n')\n57\t    .filter((path) => path.endsWith('.ts') && !NOT_PRODUCTION.test(path));\n58\t}\n59\t\n60\t/** Drop block + line comments so docstrings do not register as code. */"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"ec9d537e-1101-467b-b8cb-81912926f03f","uuid":"3f8ea68b-59ee-4e8d-8fe2-4363b48f3b75","timestamp":"2026-09-19T22:06:35.639Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-evidence-judge-a0bd4b0b5f2a/tests/invariants/no-dead-guards.spec.ts","content":"/**\n * INVARIANT — no dead guards (ADR-0010, SEC-HIGH-060).\n *\n * `IpWhitelistGuard` sat in gateway-api for two years registered in no\n * module, under no `APP_GUARD`, in no `@UseGuards()`. It read as an access\n * control and controlled nothing. A guard class that exists but is wired to\n * no request path is worse than no guard: reviewers, auditors and the next\n * engineer all read it as protection.\n *\n * Rule: every non-abstract class implementing `CanActivate` under `apps/**`\n * and `libs/**` (production source, not tests) must be reachable from a\n * registration site:\n *   - an `APP_GUARD` provider (`useClass`, `useExisting`, or a `useFactory`\n *     that constructs it),\n *   - a `@UseGuards(...)` / `UseGuards(...)` call (controller, resolver,\n *     handler, or an `applyDecorators` bundle),\n *   - a `globalGuards: [...]` / `useGlobalGuards(...)` bootstrap list,\n *   - or a registered guard that `extends` it,\n * or carry an entry in `.claude/allowlists/unregistered-guards.yaml` with\n * `{owner, expiry, findingId, reason}`. Expired entries fail; entries whose\n * class no longer exists fail (the list only shrinks).\n *\n * The second block keeps both retired IP access-rule stacks deleted.\n */\n\nimport { execFileSync } from 'node:child_process';\nimport { readFileSync } from 'node:fs';\nimport { resolve } from 'node:path';\n\nimport * as yaml from 'js-yaml';\n\nconst REPO_ROOT = resolve(__dirname, '..', '..');\nconst ALLOWLIST = resolve(REPO_ROOT, '.claude/allowlists/unregistered-guards.yaml');\n\n/** Production source only: tests, fixtures and built output are not registration evidence. */\nconst NOT_PRODUCTION =\n  /(^|\\/)(__tests__|__mocks__|test|tests|e2e|dist|\\.archive)\\/|\\.(spec|test)\\.tsx?$/;\n\nfunction listSourceFiles(): string[] {\n  return execFileSync(\n    'git',\n    [\n      '-C',\n      REPO_ROOT,\n      'ls-files',\n      '--cached',\n      '--others',\n      '--exclude-standard',\n      '--',\n      'apps',\n      'libs',\n      'platform',\n    ],\n    { encoding: 'utf8', maxBuffer: 64 * 1024 * 1024 },\n  )\n    .split('\\n')\n    .filter((path) => path.endsWith('.ts') && !NOT_PRODUCTION.test(path));\n}\n\n/** Drop block + line comments so docstrings do not register as code. */","numLines":60,"startLine":1,"totalLines":333}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01FowMfRknuieAAR7Bfk7MWu","type":"tool_result","content":"2228\t    # Plan ARIA-V7 §2g v2 + V7.10 envelope-extraction fix.\n2229\t    #\n2230\t    # WHY: claude -p stream-json emits JSONL events\n2231\t    # ({\"result\": \"...\", \"total_cost_usd\": ..., ...}) where the agent's final message may contain the\n2232\t    # actual aria/agent-response/v1 envelope as fenced ```json``` block\n2233\t    # or as final JSON block. Writing raw JSONL to output_path means\n2234\t    # kernel submit-result reads runtime telemetry instead of the envelope,\n2235\t    # which fails:\n2236\t    #   response_schema: missing required fields:\n2237\t    #     ['$schema', 'request_id', 'claim_id', 'agent_id', 'role',\n2238\t    #      'status', 'satisfaction_matrix']\n2239\t    #\n2240\t    # HOW: parse JSONL, extract the final message, find the embedded\n2241\t    # envelope JSON, INJECT mandatory identity fields ($schema,\n2242\t    # request_id, claim_id, agent_id, role, status) from the known\n2243\t    # ci_executor context (these aren't the agent's job — the agent\n2244\t    # only knows its plan content), synthesize a satisfaction_matrix\n2245\t    # from must_satisfy with verdict=satisfied when the agent omitted\n2246\t    # it, then write the corrected envelope to output_path.\n2247\t    #\n2248\t    # Tier hierarchy: Tier-2 (Make it automatic) — the envelope shape\n2249\t    # is now produced correctly by default; agents don't have to know\n2250\t    # internal kernel identity fields.\n2251\t    if completed.stdout:\n2252\t        envelope = _build_envelope_from_claude_output(\n2253\t            raw_stdout=completed.stdout,\n2254\t            request_id=request_id,\n2255\t            claim_id=claim_id or \"\",\n2256\t            agent_id=agent_id or \"\",\n2257\t            role=role,\n2258\t            subagent_type=subagent_type,\n2259\t            must_satisfy=must_satisfy or [],\n2260\t            dispatch_model=agent_profile.model,\n2261\t        )\n2262\t        envelope[\"details\"][\"agent_contract_hash\"] = agent_contract.contract_hash\n2263\t        output_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n2264\t        _write_sanitized_envelope(output_path, envelope)\n2265\t        resolved_transcript_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n2266\t        resolved_transcript_path.write_text(completed.stdout, encoding=\"utf-8\")\n2267\t        # Plan ARIA-V3.1-D3 — per-LLM-call cost attribution. Gated on"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"ec9d537e-1101-467b-b8cb-81912926f03f","uuid":"448e8772-5899-4911-bcfe-50cfaeb425a8","timestamp":"2026-09-19T22:07:36.682Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-evidence-judge-a0bd4b0b5f2a/tools/aria-poc/ci_executor.py","content":"    # Plan ARIA-V7 §2g v2 + V7.10 envelope-extraction fix.\n    #\n    # WHY: claude -p stream-json emits JSONL events\n    # ({\"result\": \"...\", \"total_cost_usd\": ..., ...}) where the agent's final message may contain the\n    # actual aria/agent-response/v1 envelope as fenced ```json``` block\n    # or as final JSON block. Writing raw JSONL to output_path means\n    # kernel submit-result reads runtime telemetry instead of the envelope,\n    # which fails:\n    #   response_schema: missing required fields:\n    #     ['$schema', 'request_id', 'claim_id', 'agent_id', 'role',\n    #      'status', 'satisfaction_matrix']\n    #\n    # HOW: parse JSONL, extract the final message, find the embedded\n    # envelope JSON, INJECT mandatory identity fields ($schema,\n    # request_id, claim_id, agent_id, role, status) from the known\n    # ci_executor context (these aren't the agent's job — the agent\n    # only knows its plan content), synthesize a satisfaction_matrix\n    # from must_satisfy with verdict=satisfied when the agent omitted\n    # it, then write the corrected envelope to output_path.\n    #\n    # Tier hierarchy: Tier-2 (Make it automatic) — the envelope shape\n    # is now produced correctly by default; agents don't have to know\n    # internal kernel identity fields.\n    if completed.stdout:\n        envelope = _build_envelope_from_claude_output(\n            raw_stdout=completed.stdout,\n            request_id=request_id,\n            claim_id=claim_id or \"\",\n            agent_id=agent_id or \"\",\n            role=role,\n            subagent_type=subagent_type,\n            must_satisfy=must_satisfy or [],\n            dispatch_model=agent_profile.model,\n        )\n        envelope[\"details\"][\"agent_contract_hash\"] = agent_contract.contract_hash\n        output_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n        _write_sanitized_envelope(output_path, envelope)\n        resolved_transcript_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n        resolved_transcript_path.write_text(completed.stdout, encoding=\"utf-8\")\n        # Plan ARIA-V3.1-D3 — per-LLM-call cost attribution. Gated on","numLines":40,"startLine":2228,"totalLines":5265}}}

Return whichever exists.\n67\t    \"\"\"\n68\t    if isinstance(details.get(\"verdict\"), dict):\n69\t        return dict(details[\"verdict\"])\n70\t    if isinstance(details.get(\"consensus\"), dict):\n71\t        return dict(details[\"consensus\"])\n72\t    if isinstance(details.get(\"verdict\"), str):\n73\t        # Flat shape (ORPHAN-HIGH-629 sibling): the judge contract file says\n74\t        # BOTH \"Return JSON with: verdict, judge_id, confidence...\" and\n75\t        # \"details.verdict.rationale\". The first live judge run answered\n76\t        # flat, was ACCEPTED by the envelope gate, and dropped HERE with\n77\t        # \"expected verdict ..., got None\" — a verdict lost between two\n78\t        # halves of one document. Honour both spellings; the envelope gate\n79\t        # stays the only authority on validity.\n80\t        return {\n81\t            key: details.get(key)\n82\t            for key in (\n83\t                \"verdict\", \"tool_id\", \"run_id\", \"finding_id\", \"judge_id\",\n84\t                \"model\", \"prompt_hash\", \"confidence\", \"rationale\",\n85\t                \"evidence_refs\", \"judgment_group_id\", \"severity\",\n86\t            )\n87\t            if details.get(key) is not None\n88\t        }\n89\t    return {}\n90\t\n91\t\n92\tdef validate_judge_response(\n93\t    *,\n94\t    request: dict[str, Any],\n95\t    response: dict[str, Any],\n96\t) -> list[str]:\n97\t    \"\"\"Y5 (ORPHAN-706) — the judge output contract, checkable BEFORE accept.\n98\t\n99\t    The second sealed night accepted 12 judge results whose envelopes never\n100\t    carried a verdict the bridge could read (\"expected verdict ..., got\n101\t    None\") — each one an accepted row that could never fold, burning bridge\n102\t    retries toward permanent_fail. This is the SINGLE definition of what a\n103\t    judge response must carry; ``record_judge_verdict_from_response`` raises\n104\t    on exactly this list, and the executor's pre-submit gate releases the\n105\t    claim on it instead of sealing an unfoldable result.\n106\t\n107\t    Returns [] for non-judge roles: the contract binds judges only.\n108\t    \"\"\"\n109\t    role = response.get(\"role\")\n110\t    if not is_judge_role(role):\n111\t        return []\n112\t    errors: list[str] = []\n113\t    details = response.get(\"details\") or {}\n114\t    if not isinstance(details, dict):\n115\t        details = {}\n116\t    verdict_block = _verdict_field(details)\n117\t    if not verdict_block:\n118\t        return [\"judge_verdict:absent\"]\n119\t    verdict = verdict_block.get(\"verdict\")\n120\t    if verdict not in FEEDBACK_VERDICTS:\n121\t        # ORPHAN-CRITICAL-735 — the arbiter's contract says \"return\n122\t        # details.consensus, OR the uncertainty reason when the consensus\n123\t        # gate cannot be met\", and the deterministic engine it mirrors\n124\t        # emits exactly that non-verdict outcome. The live arbiter obeyed\n125\t        # and this contract burned the claim as invalid:None — fourth\n126\t        # mint-vs-law instance. Uncertainty is a VALID arbitration\n127\t        # outcome, for the arbiter alone, with a reason from the closed\n128\t        # vocabulary; binary judges stay bound to binary verdicts.\n129\t        reason = verdict_block.get(\"uncertainty_reason\")"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"ec9d537e-1101-467b-b8cb-81912926f03f","uuid":"4e549c07-bf25-44bd-8e15-a35e900f4aeb","timestamp":"2026-09-19T22:08:12.846Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-evidence-judge-a0bd4b0b5f2a/aria-kernel/aria_kernel/judgment_bridge.py","content":"        return \"low\"\n    return fallback\n\n\ndef _verdict_field(details: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"The judge .md prompts shape verdicts under details.verdict (evidence,\n    adversarial) or details.consensus (arbiter). Return whichever exists.\n    \"\"\"\n    if isinstance(details.get(\"verdict\"), dict):\n        return dict(details[\"verdict\"])\n    if isinstance(details.get(\"consensus\"), dict):\n        return dict(details[\"consensus\"])\n    if isinstance(details.get(\"verdict\"), str):\n        # Flat shape (ORPHAN-HIGH-629 sibling): the judge contract file says\n        # BOTH \"Return JSON with: verdict, judge_id, confidence...\" and\n        # \"details.verdict.rationale\". The first live judge run answered\n        # flat, was ACCEPTED by the envelope gate, and dropped HERE with\n        # \"expected verdict ..., got None\" — a verdict lost between two\n        # halves of one document. Honour both spellings; the envelope gate\n        # stays the only authority on validity.\n        return {\n            key: details.get(key)\n            for key in (\n                \"verdict\", \"tool_id\", \"run_id\", \"finding_id\", \"judge_id\",\n                \"model\", \"prompt_hash\", \"confidence\", \"rationale\",\n                \"evidence_refs\", \"judgment_group_id\", \"severity\",\n            )\n            if details.get(key) is not None\n        }\n    return {}\n\n\ndef validate_judge_response(\n    *,\n    request: dict[str, Any],\n    response: dict[str, Any],\n) -> list[str]:\n    \"\"\"Y5 (ORPHAN-706) — the judge output contract, checkable BEFORE accept.\n\n    The second sealed night accepted 12 judge results whose envelopes never\n    carried a verdict the bridge could read (\"expected verdict ..., got\n    None\") — each one an accepted row that could never fold, burning bridge\n    retries toward permanent_fail. This is the SINGLE definition of what a\n    judge response must carry; ``record_judge_verdict_from_response`` raises\n    on exactly this list, and the executor's pre-submit gate releases the\n    claim on it instead of sealing an unfoldable result.\n\n    Returns [] for non-judge roles: the contract binds judges only.\n    \"\"\"\n    role = response.get(\"role\")\n    if not is_judge_role(role):\n        return []\n    errors: list[str] = []\n    details = response.get(\"details\") or {}\n    if not isinstance(details, dict):\n        details = {}\n    verdict_block = _verdict_field(details)\n    if not verdict_block:\n        return [\"judge_verdict:absent\"]\n    verdict = verdict_block.get(\"verdict\")\n    if verdict not in FEEDBACK_VERDICTS:\n        # ORPHAN-CRITICAL-735 — the arbiter's contract says \"return\n        # details.consensus, OR the uncertainty reason when the consensus\n        # gate cannot be met\", and the deterministic engine it mirrors\n        # emits exactly that non-verdict outcome. The live arbiter obeyed\n        # and this contract burned the claim as invalid:None — fourth\n        # mint-vs-law instance. Uncertainty is a VALID arbitration\n        # outcome, for the arbiter alone, with a reason from the closed\n        # vocabulary; binary judges stay bound to binary verdicts.\n        reason = verdict_block.get(\"uncertainty_reason\")","numLines":70,"startLine":60,"totalLines":388}}}

On live\n292\t        # state 7 of 8 ai_judge rows carried an empty fingerprint because\n293\t        # the mint never carried it and no prompt asks the judge to echo it,\n294\t        # so every consensus row settled from those rows inherited the empty\n295\t        # key — and promote_consensus_findings silently skipped it forever.\n296\t        finding_fingerprint=request.get(\"finding_fingerprint\") or verdict_block.get(\"finding_fingerprint\"),\n297\t        base_dir=base_dir,\n298\t    )\n299\t    return row\n300\t\n301\t\n302\tdef persist_supporting_payload(\n303\t    *,\n304\t    request: dict[str, Any],\n305\t    response: dict[str, Any],\n306\t    base_dir: str | Path | None = None,\n307\t) -> dict[str, Any] | None:\n308\t    \"\"\"Plan 016 Faz C6 — persist Goldset / Change-Intelligence outputs.\n309\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"ec9d537e-1101-467b-b8cb-81912926f03f","uuid":"092b35cb-bba4-47cf-94d2-797fc76afb56","timestamp":"2026-09-19T22:08:14.201Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-evidence-judge-a0bd4b0b5f2a/aria-kernel/aria_kernel/judgment_bridge.py","content":"    note = (\n        verdict_block.get(\"rationale\")\n        or verdict_block.get(\"note\")\n        or response.get(\"rationale\")\n        or \"envelope-routed judge submission\"\n    )\n    if not isinstance(note, str) or not note.strip():\n        note = \"envelope-routed judge submission\"\n\n    raw_evidence_refs = verdict_block.get(\"evidence_refs\") or response.get(\"evidence_refs\") or []\n    evidence_refs = [str(r) for r in raw_evidence_refs if isinstance(r, str) and r.strip()]\n\n    row = record_operator_feedback(\n        tool_id=str(tool_id),\n        run_id=str(run_id),\n        finding_id=str(finding_id),\n        verdict=verdict,\n        severity=severity,\n        note=note,\n        source_type=\"ai_judge\",\n        judge_id=str(judge_id),\n        # ORPHAN-HIGH-781 — mint-first doctrine, same as judgment_group_id\n        # and finding_fingerprint above: the model the DISPATCHER resolved\n        # (`details.agent_dispatch_model`, force-stamped by ci_executor from\n        # the runtime profile) outranks the judge's own verdict.model\n        # self-report. The anchor distinct-model count must not trust a\n        # string the judged agent wrote about itself; the self-report\n        # survives only as the fallback for mock/legacy envelopes that\n        # predate the stamp.\n        model=details.get(\"agent_dispatch_model\") or verdict_block.get(\"model\"),\n        prompt_hash=verdict_block.get(\"prompt_hash\"),\n        confidence=float(confidence) if confidence is not None else None,\n        rationale=str(note),\n        evidence_refs=evidence_refs,\n        # D1 — group identity comes from the MINT (judge_fanout's canonical\n        # `judge:<tool>:<run>:<finding>`), never from the judge's own payload:\n        # a judge echoing its request id as group id split one finding's two\n        # verdicts across two groups-of-one, so the real disagreement on\n        # anthropic.provider.ts:3 never met itself and never escalated.\n        judgment_group_id=request.get(\"judgment_group_id\") or verdict_block.get(\"judgment_group_id\"),\n        # ORPHAN-HIGH-765 — the fingerprint follows the SAME D1 doctrine as\n        # the group id above: mint first, envelope only as fallback. On live\n        # state 7 of 8 ai_judge rows carried an empty fingerprint because\n        # the mint never carried it and no prompt asks the judge to echo it,\n        # so every consensus row settled from those rows inherited the empty\n        # key — and promote_consensus_findings silently skipped it forever.\n        finding_fingerprint=request.get(\"finding_fingerprint\") or verdict_block.get(\"finding_fingerprint\"),\n        base_dir=base_dir,\n    )\n    return row\n\n\ndef persist_supporting_payload(\n    *,\n    request: dict[str, Any],\n    response: dict[str, Any],\n    base_dir: str | Path | None = None,\n) -> dict[str, Any] | None:\n    \"\"\"Plan 016 Faz C6 — persist Goldset / Change-Intelligence outputs.\n","numLines":60,"startLine":250,"totalLines":388}}}

errors.append(\"judge_verdict:executor_shaped_judge_identity\")\n    return errors\n\n\ndef record_judge_verdict_from_response(\n    *,\n    request: dict[str, Any],\n    response: dict[str, Any],\n    base_dir: str | Path | None = None,\n) -> dict[str, Any] | None:\n    \"\"\"Translate an ACCEPTED judge response into an ai_judge feedback row.\n\n    Returns the persisted row, or None when the envelope role is not a\n    judge role (the bridge is a no-op for non-judge submissions).\n\n    Raises GovernanceError when the response is missing the data the\n    consensus engine requires (tool_id / run_id / finding_id come from\n    the request envelope's optional fields; verdict + severity from\n    the response.details).\n    \"\"\"\n    role = response.get(\"role\")\n    if not is_judge_role(role):\n        return None\n\n    # Y5 (ORPHAN-706) — one contract, two consumers: the executor's\n    # pre-submit gate checks exactly this list and releases instead of\n    # sealing an unfoldable accepted result; the bridge raises on it so a\n    # row that somehow slipped past still fails loudly, never half-folds.\n    contract_errors = validate_judge_response(request=request, response=response)\n    if contract_errors:\n        raise GovernanceError(\n            \"judge bridge contract violation: \" + \"; \".join(contract_errors)\n        )\n\n    details = response.get(\"details\") or {}\n    if not isinstance(details, dict):\n        details = {}\n\n    # ORPHAN-CRITICAL-735 — the arbiter's legitimate non-verdict outcome\n    # folds through the SAME producer, ledger and idempotent escalation_id\n    # as the deterministic engine, so one sweep drains both lanes into one\n    # HUMAN_REQUIRED record. It is NOT a feedback verdict: an uncertain\n    # arbitration must never write an ai_judge row.\n    if role == \"consensus_arbitration\":\n        arb_block = _verdict_field(details)\n        arb_reason = arb_block.get(\"uncertainty_reason\")\n        if arb_block.get(\"verdict\") is None and arb_reason in CONSENSUS_UNCERTAINTY_REASONS:\n            from .feedback_store import record_consensus_uncertainty\n\n            row = record_consensus_uncertainty(\n                tool_id=str(request.get(\"tool_id\") or arb_block.get(\"tool_id\") or \"\"),\n                run_id=str(request.get(\"run_id\") or arb_block.get(\"run_id\") or \"\"),\n                finding_id=str(request.get(\"finding_id\") or arb_block.get(\"finding_id\") or \"\"),\n                group_id=str(request.get(\"judgment_group_id\") or arb_block.get(\"group_id\") or \"\"),\n                reason=str(arb_reason),\n                cycle_id=request.get(\"cycle_id\"),\n                base_dir=base_dir,\n            )\n            return {\"kind\": \"consensus_uncertainty\", **row}\n    verdict_block = _verdict_field(details)\n\n    tool_id = request.get(\"tool_id\") or verdict_block.get(\"tool_id\")\n    run_id = request.get(\"run_id\") or verdict_block.get(\"run_id\")\n    finding_id = request.get(\"finding_id\") or verdict_block.get(\"finding_id\")\n    if not (tool_id and run_id and finding_id):\n        raise GovernanceError(\n            \"judge bridge requires tool_id, run_id, finding_id in request or response.details.verdict; \"\n            f\"got tool_id={tool_id!r}, run_id={run_id!r}, finding_id={finding_id!r}\"\n        )\n\n    verdict = verdict_block.get(\"verdict\")\n    if verdict not in FEEDBACK_VERDICTS:\n        raise GovernanceError(\n            f\"judge bridge expected verdict in {FEEDBACK_VERDICTS}, got {verdict!r}\"\n        )\n    severity = _coerce_severity(verdict_block.get(\"severity\") or details.get(\"severity\"))\n    # D1 (Kapalı Döngü) — judge identity. `response[\"agent_id\"]` is the\n    # EXECUTOR's identity (`ci-executor:gha-<run>`), not the judge's: reading\n    # it collapsed every judge drained by one workflow run into a single\n    # voter (consensus structurally impossible) while judges drained by\n    # DIFFERENT runs counted as distinct voters with meaningless identity.\n    # The judge's real identity is the subagent the executor invoked\n    # (`details.agent_subagent_type`, force-stamped by ci_executor); the\n    # mock lane and legacy envelopes carry it as `verdict.judge_id`.\n    judge_id = details.get(\"agent_subagent_type\") or verdict_block.get(\"judge_id\")\n    if not judge_id:\n        raise GovernanceError(\n            \"judge bridge requires details.agent_subagent_type \"\n            \"(or details.verdict.judge_id)\"\n        )\n    if str(judge_id).startswith(\"ci-executor:\"):\n        # Refuse loudly instead of silently repairing: an executor-shaped\n        # identity means the producer regressed, and a repaired row would\n        # hide that while still poisoning per-judge calibration.\n        raise GovernanceError(\n            f\"judge bridge refuses executor-shaped judge identity: {judge_id!r}\"\n        )\n\n    # The unit-interval gate, not a bare isinstance: True is an int and\n    # float(\"nan\") is a float to isinstance, and both would ride a judge\n    # verdict into calibration as if they were probabilities.\n    confidence = confidence_in_unit_interval(verdict_block.get(\"confidence\"))\n\n    note = (","numLines":122,"startLine":129,"totalLines":388}}}

}\n218\t  }\n219\t\n220\t  result.observations.push({\n221\t    id: 'test-gap-coverage-summary',\n222\t    type: 'test_gap_coverage_summary',\n223\t    details: {\n224\t      sourceFiles: /(\\.guard|\\.strategy|\\/auth\\/|\\/security\\/|\\/permission)/i.test(path);\n273\t  const writeBoundary = /\\/migrations\\/.*\\.ts$/.test(path)\n275\t    && /\\b(DROP|DELETE|TRUNCATE|ALTER\\s+TYPE|ENABLE\\s+ROW\\s+LEVEL\\s+SECURITY|FORCE\\s+ROW\\s+LEVEL\\s+SECURITY)\\b/i.test(text);\n276\t  return { return 'migration';\n282\t  }\n283\t  // match[1] is narrowed explicitly so the file stays clean under\n284\t  // --noUncheckedIndexedAccess (the capture group is structurally guaranteed,\n285\t  // but the index signature cannot prove it to the compiler).\n286\t  const match = path.match(/\\.([a-z-]+)\\.(ts|tsx)$/);\n287\t  if (match?.[1] !== undefined) {\n288\t    return match[1];\n289\t return true;\n314\t    }\n315\t    return importedSpecifiers(test.sourceFile).some((specifier) => {\n316\t      const resolved = resolveImportSpecifier(test.relativePath, specifier, sourcePaths, specifier.slice(alias.prefix.length, specifier.length - alias.suffix.length);\n362\t      for (const target of alias.targets) {\n363\t        candidates.push(target.replace('*', middle));\n364\t      }\n365\t    }\n366\t  }\n367\t  for (const candidate of candidates.flatMap(candidatePathVariants)) {\n368\t    if (sourcePaths.has(candidate)) {\n369\t      return candidate;\n370\t    }\n371\t  }\n372\t  return undefined;\n373\t}\n374\t\n375\tfunction candidatePathVariants(candidate: string): string[] {\n376\t  const normalized = normalizePath(candidate).replace(/^\\.\\//, '');\n377\t ];\n384\t}\n385\t\n386\tfunction readPathAliases(workspaceRoot: string): readonly PathAlias[] {\n387\t  const path = resolve(workspaceRoot, 'tsconfig.base.json');\n388\t  if path,\n425\t    relativePath: normalizePath(relative(workspaceRoot, path)),\n426\t    text,\n427\t    sourceFile: ts.createSourceFile(path, text, ts.ScriptTarget.Latest, true),\n428\t    isTest: !isArchivedWorkspacePath(path),\n440\t  });\n441\t}\n442\t\n443\tfunction isTestFile(path: string): boolean {\n444\t  const normalized = normalizePath(path);\n445\t  return string;\n  readonly relativePath: string;\n  readonly text: string;\n  readonly sourceFile: ts.SourceFile;\n  readonly isTest: boolean;\n}\n\ninterface SourceRisk {\n  readonly riskClass: string;\n readonly string[];\n}\n\n\nexport function analyzeTestGaps(input: AdapterInput, workspaceRoot = process.cwd()): AriaOutput {\n  const roots = requireScanRoots('test-gap-adapter', input.roots);\n  const allowlist = new Set((input.allowlist ?? []).map(normalizePath));\n  const files = roots\n    .map((root) => resolveInsideWorkspace(workspaceRoot, root))\n    .filter((root) => workspacePathExists(root))\n    .flatMap((root) => collectSourceAndTestFiles(root));\n  const snapshotFiles = filterFilesBySnapshot(files, workspaceRoot, input);\n  const units = snapshotFiles.map((file) => readFileUnit(file, workspaceRoot));\n  const tests = units.filter((unit) => unit.isTest);\n  const sources = units.filter((unit) => !unit.isTest);\n  const sourcePaths = new Set(sources.map((source) => source.relativePath));\n  const pathAliases = readPathAliases(workspaceRoot);\n  const result: AnalysisResult = {\n    observations: [],\n    findings: [],\n    readPaths: units.map((unit) => unit.relativePath),\n  };\n\n  for (const test of tests) {\n    result.observations.push({\n      id: `test-gap-test-file:${test.relativePath}`,\n      type: 'test_gap_test_file',\n      path: test.relativePath,\n      line: 1,\n      name: basename(test.relativePath),\n      details: {\n        imports: importedSpecifiers(test.sourceFile).sort(),\n      },\n    });\n  }\n\n  let highRiskWithoutTest = 0;\n  let directMatchCount = 0;\n  let weakMatchCount = 0;\n  for (const source of sources) {\n    const risk = classifySourceRisk(source);\n    if (!risk.highRisk && !risk.securitySensitive && !risk.migrationHazard) {\n      continue;\n    }\n    const matchedTests = matchingTests(source, tests, sourcePaths, pathAliases);\n    const weakMatchedTests = weakSymbolTests(source, tests).filter(\n      (test) => !matchedTests.some((matched) => matched.relativePath === test.relativePath),\n    );\n    directMatchCount += matchedTests.length > 0 ? 1 : 0;\n    weakMatchCount += weakMatchedTests.length > 0 ? 1 : 0;\n    const allowlisted = allowlist.has(source.relativePath);\n    result.observations.push({\n      id: `test-gap-source-file:${source.relativePath}`,\n      type: 'test_gap_source_file',\n      path: source.relativePath,\n      line: 1,\n      name: basename(source.relativePath),\n      details: {\n        riskClass: risk.riskClass,\n        highRisk: risk.highRisk,\n        securitySensitive: risk.securitySensitive,\n        migrationHazard: risk.migrationHazard,\n        writeBoundary: risk.writeBoundary,\n        matchedTests: matchedTests.map((test) => test.relativePath).sort(),\n        weakMatchedTests: weakMatchedTests.map((test) => test.relativePath).sort(),\n        allowlisted,\n      },\n    });\n    if (allowlisted || matchedTests.length > 0) {\n      continue;\n    }\n    if (risk.migrationHazard) {\n      highRiskWithoutTest += 1;\n      result.findings.push({\n        id: `migration-without-test:${source.relativePath}`,\n        rule: 'migration_without_test',\n        severity: 'high',\n        path: source.relativePath,\n        message: 'Hazardous migration has no adjacent or importing test coverage signal.',\n        evidence: [{ path: source.relativePath }],\n        confidence: weakMatchedTests.length > 0 ? 0.55 : 0.88,\n        actionability: weakMatchedTests.length > 0 ? 'review_required' : 'actionable',\n        review_reason: weakMatchedTests.length > 0 ? 'weak_symbol_test_match' : undefined,\n        details: { riskClass: risk.riskClass, weakMatchedTests: weakMatchedTests.map((test) => test.relativePath).sort() },\n      });\n      continue;\n    }\n    if (risk.securitySensitive) {\n      highRiskWithoutTest += 1;\n      result.findings.push({\n        id: `security-source-without-test:${source.relativePath}`,\n        rule: 'security_source_without_security_test',\n        severity: 'high',\n        path: source.relativePath,\n        message: 'Security-sensitive source file has no adjacent or importing test coverage signal.',\n        evidence: [{ path: source.relativePath }],\n        confidence: weakMatchedTests.length > 0 ? 0.55 : 0.86,\n        actionability: weakMatchedTests.length > 0 ? 'weak_symbol_test_match' : undefined,\n        details: { riskClass: risk.riskClass, weakMatchedTests: weakMatchedTests.map((test) => test.relativePath).sort() },\n      });\n      continue;\n    }\n    if (input.includeWriteBoundaryFindings === true && risk.highRisk && risk.writeBoundary) {\n      highRiskWithoutTest += 1;\n      result.findings.push({\n        id: `high-risk-source-without-adjacent-test:${source.relativePath}`,\n        rule: 'high_risk_source_without_adjacent_test',\n        severity: 'medium',\n        path: source.relativePath,\n        message: 'High-risk source file has no adjacent, sibling __tests__, or importing test coverage signal.',\n        evidence: [{ path: source.relativePath }],\n        confidence: weakMatchedTests.length > 0 ? 0.5 : 0.75,\n        actionability: weakMatchedTests.length > 0 ?
